Employee Attrition Analysis

Approach

I will be analyzing a dataset that contains information about employee attrition.

I plan to compare data to find the strongest correlations that would help organizations develop retention strategies. I will use summary statistics and visualizations to identify patterns and compare attrition rates across different employee and workplace characteristics.

Data Source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Esserac3/Data-607/refs/heads/main/employee_attrition_performance.csv

Citation: Perea Vega, C. (2026). Employee Attrition Performance [Data set]. Kaggle. https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/carlospereavega/employee-attrition-performance

Code Base

I want to compare age and attrition in a boxplot

ggplot(data = my_data, mapping = aes(x=age, y= attrition))+geom_boxplot()

Look at salary versus age versus attrition

my_data %>%
  select(age, monthly_salary, job_satisfaction, attrition) %>%
  group_by(age, attrition)%>%
  filter(mean(monthly_salary)>100000)%>%
  arrange(desc(monthly_salary))

What is the average years worked for those that left the company

my_data %>%
  filter((attrition=="Yes"))%>%
  summarise(mean_years_worked=mean(years_at_company))
  mean_years_worked
1          2.471429

Conclusion

I have analyzed the data searching for different connections. I created a boxplot to show if age was a major factor. Then completed some other functions to find other connections. To expand on my data, I would find compare department to job satisfaction to see if this is a strong factor is employee attrition. I found that the average time someone worked for the company was 2 years. The data shows that people that left the company is skewed to a younger age.
